m = Tk()

ttk.Label(m, text=”Choose the color:”).grid(column=1, row=0)

n = ttk.Combobox(m, width=12) #3

n[‘values’] = (“red”,”green”,”blue”,”orange”,”yellow”)

n.grid(column=1, row=1)

n.current(0)

mainloop()

Output

16.17 Scale Widget

The Scale widget allows the user to give a numerically bounded input and use the scale bar. It is similar to Entry widget but it will select the data by using the scale bar.

from tkinter import *

x1.pack()

x2 = Scale(m, from_=0, to=6, orient=HORIZONTAL)

x2.pack()

x3 = Scale(m, from_=0, to=7, orient=VERTICAL)

x3.pack()

mainloop()

Output

16.18 Canvas Widget

The Canvas widget is used to draw the plots and graphics in Tkinter. It is used for designing the various custom widgets.

from tkinter import *

x = Canvas(m, width=200, height=100)

x.pack()

x.create_line(0, 0, 200, 100)

x.create_rectangle(50, 25, 150, 75, fill=”red”)

x.create_oval(50, 20, 100, 75, fill=”blue”)

mainloop()

Output

Glossary

■ Tkinter is a package used by Python to support Graphical User Interface (GUI).

■ Widget represents objects for handling GUI.

Summary

The Tkinter supports design of various GUI based applications. For each widget, create an object and then place it in the corresponding GUI window using pack(), grid() or place() methods. With this, various widgets such as label, button, entry, listbox, etc., can be created and placed in the GUI application window.

CHAPTER

  

Events

17

  

The present chapter discusses about advanced concepts of GUI programming. This chapter elaborates on handling events in Python. An event is an object connected to a thread. With Python, the mouse event and key events can be handled efficiently.

17.1 Event Object

An event object can handle various program inputs. Python uses event objects to handle inputs. Among them popular inputs are

■ Thread

■ Widget

An event object for the thread will maintain an internal flag. All the threads in a program can be connected to the event. Any thread can use the event object. The event object for the thread is available in threading class.

import threading

import time

class newthread(threading.Thread):

def __init__(self,tname,event):

threading.Thread.__init__(self)

self.name=tname

self.threadEvent=event

def run(self):

print(self.name,” thread is running”)

e=threading.Event()

t1=newthread(“t1”,e)

t2=newthread(“t2”,e)

t1.start()

t2.start()

e.clear()

print(“The thread event object is cleared”)

time.sleep(5)

e.set()

print(“the thread event object is set”)

Output

D:\PythonPrograms>python a1.py

t1 thread is running

t2 thread is running

The thread event object is cleared

the thread event object is set

Various methods for the thread event object are listed below.

■ set() is used to set the internal flag of the thread

■ clear() is used to clear the internal flag of the thread

■ wait(time_period) is used to block the internal flag of the thread for a specified amount of time.

■ isSet() is used to check whether the internal flag value is set or not.

The event object can be applied to the tkinter widgets also. For this, the attribute of the event object should be a widget. The widget attribute indicates the widget for which the event object is created. The x,y attributes indicates the position of mouse. The x_root,y_root attributes indicates the relative position of mouse from top left corner position. The char attribute indicates the key value given from the keyboard. The keysym attribute indicates the key symbol given from the keyboard. The keycode attribute indicates the key code given from the keyboard. The num attribute indicates the number of the button pressed by the mouse. The width indicates the width of the input widget. The height indicates the height of the input widget. The type indicates the type of the widget.

17.2 Binding Callbacks to Events

In Python, binding is a process to connect the widget event object to the corresponding callback. In general, this is also called as “Event Handler”. For this, the tkinter module contains a method called bind(). The bind() method is applicable in four different cases

■ Applicable to widget instance

In this, the bind() is applied on the instance. The syntax of the bind() method is

widget_object.bind(event_name,handler)

from tkinter import *

a = Tk()

def abc(event):

x=event.widget

f=Frame(a)

f.pack()

l1=Label(f, text=x).pack()

t1 = Text(a, width=30, height=10)

t1.bind(‘<KeyPress>’, abc)

t1.pack()

t1.focus_set()

a.mainloop()

Output

■ Applicable to top level window

In this, the bind() method is applied on the top level window in the GUI application. The syntax of the bind() is

top_level_object.bind(event_name,handler)

from tkinter import *

a = Tk()

def abc(event):

print(“mouse entered”)

frame = Frame(a, width=200, height=200)

frame.bind(‘<Enter>’, abc)

frame.pack()

a.mainloop()

Output

■ Applicable to widget class

In this case, the bind_class() method is used. This will perform binding operation for all widgets of the same class. the bind_class() method is applied on the top-level window in the GUI application. The syntax of the bind_class() is

top_level_object.bind_class(“widget_class”, event_name,handler)

from tkinter import *

a = Tk()

def abc(event):

x=event.widget

f=Frame(a)

f.pack()

l1=Label(f, text=x).pack()

b1 = Button(a, text=”Button1”)

b1.bind_class(‘Button’,’<Button-1>’, abc)

b1.pack()

b2 = Button(a, text=”Button2”)

b2.bind_class(‘Button’,’<Button-1>’, abc)

b2.pack()

a.mainloop()

Output

■ Applicable to complete GUI application

In this case, bind_all() is used. This will perform binding process for all the widgets in the GUI application. The syntax for the bind_all() is given below.

top_level_object.bind_all(event_name,handler)

from tkinter import *

a = Tk()

def abc(event):

x=event.widget

f=Frame(a)

f.pack()

l1=Label(f, text=x).pack()

b1 = Button(a, text=”Button1”)

b1.pack()

b2 = Button(a, text=”Button2”)

b2.pack()

a.bind_all(‘<Button-1>’, abc)

a.mainloop()

Output

17.3 Events Names

The various event names in Python are listed below.

o Button-1

o B1-Motion

o ButtonRelease-1

o Double-Button-1

o Enter

o Leave

o FocusIn

o FocusOut

o Return

o Key

o <Shift-Up>

o <Configure>

17.4 Keyboard Events

The keyboard events will ‘listen’ to the state of input from the keyboard and whenever an event occurs, the associated action will be performed.

from tkinter import *

a = Tk()

def abc(event):

x=”Key Symbol: “+event.keysym

y=”Key Symbol Number: “+str(event.keysym_num)

f=Frame(a)

f.pack()

l4=Label(f, text=”Key Pressed”).pack()

l1=Label(f, text=x).pack()

l2=Label(f, text=y).pack()

def abc1(event):

f=Frame(a)

f.pack()

l4=Label(f, text=”Key Released”).pack()

text = Text(a, width=20, height=5)

text.bind(‘<KeyPress>’, abc)

text.bind(‘<KeyRelease>’, abc1)

text.pack(expand=1, fill=”both”)

text.focus_set()

a.mainloop()

Output

17.5 Mouse Events

Mouse events are used to handle changes in the mouse and to perform the corresponding action.

from tkinter import *

a = Tk()

def abc(event):

x=str(event.x)

y=str(event.y)

x1=str(event.x_root)

y1=str(event.y_root)

f=Frame(a)

f.pack()

l4=Label(f, text=”Mouse clicked at (“+x+”,”+y+”)”).pack()

l5=Label(f, text=”Mouse relative position(“+x1+”,”+y1+”)”).pack()

def abc1(event):

f=Frame(a)

f.pack()

l4=Label(f, text=”Mouse Entered”).pack()

def abc2(event):

f=Frame(a)

f.pack()

l4=Label(f, text=”Mouse Leaved”).pack()

def abc3(event):

f=Frame(a)

f.pack()

l4=Label(f, text=”Mouse Dragged”).pack()

def abc4(event):

f=Frame(a)

f.pack()

l4=Label(f, text=”Mouse Released”).pack()

a.bind(‘<Enter>’,abc1)

a.bind(‘<Button-1>’,abc)

a.bind(‘<Leave>’,abc2)

a.bind(‘<B1-Motion>’,abc3)

a.bind(‘<ButtonRelease-1>’,abc4)

a.mainloop()

Output

17.6 Sample Programs

Program 1: Write a Python program to wait the thread event object for a specified time.

Programming Tip:

Event object handles various inputs.

import threading

import time

class newthread(threading.Thread):

def __init__(self,tname,event):

threading.Thread.__init__(self)

self.name=tname

self.threadEvent=event

def run(self):

print(self.name,” thread is running”)

e=threading.Event()

t1=newthread(“t1”,e)

t2=newthread(“t2”,e)

t1.start()

t2.start()

x=int(input(“Enter the time period for wait: “))

print(“The thread event object is waiting”)

e.wait(x)

print(“The thread event object is cleared”)

Output

D:\PythonPrograms>python a1.py

t1 thread is running

t2 thread is running

Enter the time period for wait: 5

The thread event object is waiting

The thread event object is cleared

Program 2: Write a Python program to print whether the mouse click is at right or left.

from tkinter import *

a = Tk()

def abc(event):

f=Frame(a)

f.pack()

l1=Label(f, text=”Left is clicked”).pack()

def abc1(event):

f=Frame(a)

f.pack()

l1=Label(f, text=”Middle is clicked”).pack()

def abc2(event):

f=Frame(a)

f.pack()

l1=Label(f, text=”Right is clicked”).pack()

a.bind(‘<Button-1>’,abc)

a.bind(‘<Button-2>’,abc1)

a.bind(‘<Button-3>’,abc2)

a.mainloop()

Output

Glossary

■ An event object can handle various program inputs.

■ Binding is a process to connect the widget event object to the corresponding callback.

■ The keyboard events will ‘listen’ to the state of input from the keyboard and whenever an event occurs then the associated action will be performed.

■ Mouse events are used to handle the various changes in the mouse and can perform the corresponding action.

Summary

■ An event object can handle various types of objects. Binding is a process to connect the callback to the event object.

■ The event object can bind four different cases in Python.

Standard Library

18

  

18.1 Operating System Interface

Python programming runs under an operating system. It contains a feature to communicate with the operating system. These features are available in os module. So, in any Python program, import os statement is required to communicate with the operating system. The various functions defined in the os module are listed below.

■ The system() function is sued to execute any operating system command. For this function, the command to be executed should be passed as an argument. The command should be specified as a string.

import os

os.system(“dir m*.*”)

Output:

D:\PythonPrograms>python a1.py

Volume in drive D has no label.

Volume Serial Number is 4006-8C06

Directory of D:\PythonPrograms

05/17/201 12:18  275 main.py

05/11/201 12:24 611 math functions.py

05/11/201 02:41 152 member op.py

3 File(s) 1,038 bytes

0 Dir(s) 271,706,546,176 bytes free

■ getcwd() function returns the current working directory.

import os

print(“Name of OS:”, os.name)

print(“Current working directory: “, os.getcwd())

Output

D:\PythonPrograms>python a1.py

Name of OS: nt

Current working directory: D:\PythonPrograms

■ mkdir(dir_name) function creates a new directory with the name dir_name in the current directory.

import os

print(“Creating D1 directory: “,os.mkdir(“dir1”))

Output

■ getpid() function returns the process id of the current process.

■ chdir(“path_dir”) function changes the current directory to path_dir.

18.2 Text Processing

Python contains standard libraries for providing services for text processing. The various modules used for text processing are listed below.

■ string module is used to handle string objects.

■ re module is used to handle regular expressions and pattern matching.

■ difflib module is used to compute the deltas.

o The SequenceMatcher class is used to match sequence pairs of text.

o The Differ class is used to compare lines of text. The Differ delta contains three possible symbols, viz., ‘- ‘, ‘+ ‘, ‘? ‘.

o The HtmlDiff class is used to compare text side by side and line by line and display the result in a HTML table.

o The context_diff class is used to compare the given string lists and the result is placed in the format of context diff.

o The get_close_matches() returns a perfect matched result.

o The ndiff() compares the given string lists and returns the differ delta.

■ textwrap module is used to wrap and fill the text.

o The wrap() wraps each and every line of the string to make the line width uniform.

o The fill() wraps each and every line of the string to make the line width uniform and returns the wrapped text.

o The shorten() will shorten or reduce the input text by removing the exceeded text.

o The dedent() removes all leading whitespaces in each and every line.

o The intent() adds prefix data to the specified lines of text.

■ unicodedata module is used for Unicode Database (UCD), which contains all character properties.

o The lookup() returns the character of the input string.

o The name() returns the name of the character.

o The decimal() returns the input character decimal value.

o The digit() returns the digit value of the input integer character.

o The numeric() returns the numeric value of the input float character.

18.3 Mathematics

Python contains standard libraries for handling numeric and mathematical data. Various modules used for this are listed below.

■ The numbers module is used to handle number data.

■ The math module is used to handle the various mathematical functions.

■ The cmath module is used to handle the various mathematical functions for complex number data.

o The phase() function returns the phase of the input complex number.

o The polar() function returns the polar form of the input complex number.

o The exp() function returns the exponential value of the input complex number.

o The log() function returns the logarithmic value of the input complex number.

o The log10() function returns the base-10 logarithm value of the input complex number.

o The acos() returns the arc cosine value of the input complex number.

o The atanh() returns the inverse hyperbolic tangent value of the input complex number.

import cmath

a=complex(10,5)

print(“Input number: “,a)

print(“Phase: “,cmath.phase(a))

print(“polar: “,cmath.polar(a))

print(“log: “,cmath.log(a))

Output

D:\PythonPrograms>python a1.py

Input number: (10+5j)

Phase: 0.4636476090008061

polar: (11.180339887498949, 0.4636476090008061)

log: (2.4141568686511508+0.4636476090008061j)

■ The decimal module is used to handle various arithmetic operations of decimal fixed point and floating point data.

o The getcontext() returns the current context.

o The Decimal() function is used to create a decimal object.

o The as_integer_ratio() function will represent the floating point data as a pair of integers.

o The as_tuple() function is used to represent the input number into a tuple with sign, digit and exponent component.

o The compare() function is used to compare two input decimal objects.

o The copy_abs() function is used to compute the absolute value of the input number.

o The copy_negate() function is used to compute the negative value of the input number.

import decimal

print(decimal.getcontext())

a=decimal.Decimal(‘5.1’)

print(“Decimal object= “,a)

print(“Result of as_integer_ratio()= “,a.as_integer_ratio())

print(“Result of as_tuple()= “,a.as_tuple())

print(“Result of copy_abs()= “,a.copy_abs())

Output

D:\PythonPrograms>python a1.py

Context(prec=28, rounding=ROUND_HALF_EVEN, Emin=-999999, Emax=999999, capitals=1, clamp=0, flags=[], traps=[InvalidOperation, DivisionByZero, Overflow])

Decimal object= 5.1

Result of as_integer_ratio()= (51, 10)

Result of as_tuple()= DecimalTuple(sign=0, digits=(5, 1), exponent=-1)

Result of copy_abs()= 5.1

■ The fractions module is used to handle the operations on rational number data.

o The Fraction() function is used to create an object for the Fraction from the input arguments.

o The limit_denominator() function represents the input fraction denominator to the input max limit.

o The __floor__() function returns the greatest integer value of the input fractional number.

o The __ceil__() function returns the least integer value of the input fractional number.

o The __round__() function returns the rounded value of the input fractional number.

import fractions

a=fractions.Fraction(35634,7568)

print(“Fraction object= “,a)

print(“Result of limit_denominator: “,a.limit_denominator(2))

print(“Result of __floor__(): “,a.__floor__())

print(“Result of __ceil__(): “,a.__ceil__())

print(“Result of __round__(): “,a.__round__())

Output

D:\PythonPrograms>python a1.py

Fraction object= 17817/3784

Result of limit_denominator: 9/2

Result of __floor__(): 4

Result of __ceil__(): 5

Result of __round__(): 5

■ The random module is used to handle the operations on pseudo random number data.

o The getrandbits() function is used to generate a random number with the specified number of bits.

o The random() function is used to generate random floating value data.

o The randrange() function generates a random integer number within the specified range.

o The shuffle() function will shuffle all the items in the list.

import random

print(“Result of random(): “,random.random())

print(“Result of randrange(): “,random.randrange(30))

print(“Result of getrandbits(): “,random.getrandbits(6))

b=[1, 2, 43, 9]

print(“Original list: “,b)

random.shuffle(b)

print(“Result of shuffle: “,b)

Output

D:\PythonPrograms>python a1.py

Result of random(): 0.2619957151196758

Result of randrange(): 18

Result of getrandbits(): 63

Original list: [1, 2, 43, 9]

Result of shuffle: [2, 9, 1, 43]

■ The statistics module is used to handle various statistical operations.

o The mean() function is used to compute the mean of the input data.

o The median() function is used to compute the median of the input data.

o The mode() function is used to compute the mode of the input data.

o The stdev() function is used to compute the standard deviation of the input data.

o The variance() function is used to compute the variance of the input data.

import statistics

x=[45,21,93,73]

print(“Input data: “,x)

print(“Result of mean(): “,statistics.mean(x))

print(“Result of median(): “,statistics.median(x))

print(“Result of stdev(): “,statistics.stdev(x))

print(“Result of variance(): “,statistics.variance(x))

Output

D:\PythonPrograms>python a1.py

Input data: [45, 21, 93, 73]

Result of mean(): 58

Result of median(): 59.0

Result of stdev(): 31.559467676119

Result of variance(): 996

18.4 Internet Access

In Python, there are two ways to access the internet. They are

■ Open the required URL in the command line interface

o For this, urllib package should be used. In this package, there are four modules available, namely, request, error, parse and robotparser.

o Among these four modules, request module is used for opening the input URL.

import urllib.request

f = urllib.request.urlopen(«http://www.google.org»).read()

print(f)

Output

D:\PythonPrograms>python a1.py

b’<!DOCTYPE html>\n<html lang=”en_US”>\n <head>\n <meta charset=”utf-8”>\n <meta content=”IE=Edge” http-equiv=”X-UA-Compatible”>\n <!-- Google Tag Manager -->\n <script>(function(w,d,s,l,i){w[l]=w[l]||[];w[l].push({\’gtm.start\’:\n new Date().getTime(),event:\’gtm.js\’});var f=d.getElementsByTagName(s)[0],\n j=d.createElement(s),dl=l!=\’dataLayer\’?\’&l=\’+l:\’\’;j.async=true;j.src=\n

...................................................

■ Open the required URL in the web browser

o For this case, webbrowser module will be used.

import webbrowser

webbrowser.open(«http:://www.google.com»)

Output

D:\PythonPrograms>python a2.py

18.5 Dates and Times

Python contains datetime module for handling the various operations of date and time. Naïve and aware objects of date and time are allowed in Python. The aware object has pre knowledge about the input whereas the naïve object has no pre knowledge. Various methods in this module are

■ The MINYEAR represents the minimum year allowed.

■ The MAXYEAR represents the maximum year allowed.

■ The timedelta object represents the duration difference of date or time.

■ The date() creates a date with the input year, month and day.

■ The today() returns the current date.

import datetime

d=datetime.date(2011,10,6)

print(datetime.MINYEAR)

print(datetime.MAXYEAR)

print(“The date= “,d)

print(“today= “,d.today())

Output:

D:\PythonPrograms>python a1.py

1

9999

The date= 2011-10-06

today= 2018-02-22

18.6 Data Compression

In Python, data is compressed by using any one of the following algorithms.

■ zlib

■ gzip

■ bz2

■ lzma

■ zipfile

■ tarfile

In this section we discuss about zipfile based data compression in Python. For this, the zipfile module should be imported. It can compress the files within 4MB size. The various functions are

■ The is_zipfile() checks whether the input file exists or not.

■ The Zipfile() is used to create a new zip file.

■ The namelist() returns all the files available in the input zip file

■ The infolist() will return the information about the zip file.

The below program creates a zipfile with two text files.

import zipfile

a = zipfile.ZipFile(‘ex1.zip’, ‘w’)

a.write(“one.txt”)

a.write(“two.txt”)

print(a.namelist())

Output

D:\PythonPrograms>python a1.py

[‘one.txt’, ‘two.txt’]

Note: Make sure that “one.txt” and “two.txt” files are available in the current directory.

■ The read() is used to read the content of file in zip file.

import zipfile

a = zipfile.ZipFile(‘ex1.zip’)

c=a.read(“one.txt”)

print(repr(c))

c=a.read(“two.txt”)

print(repr(c))

Output

D:\PythonPrograms>python a1.py

b’this is one.txt file’

b’this is two.txt file’

■ The getinfo() will return the information of a file in zip file.

import zipfile

a = zipfile.ZipFile(‘ex1.zip’)

i=a.getinfo(“one.txt”)

print(“Name of the file: “,i.filename)

i=a.getinfo(“two.txt”)

print(“Name of the file: “,i.filename)

Output

D:\PythonPrograms>python a1.py

Name of the file: one.txt

Name of the file: two.txt

18.7 Turtle Graphics

The “turtle” is a module used to support graphics in Python. For this, turtle module should be imported in the Python program. Various functions in this module are

■ Screen() – Creates a screen for the turtle

■ forward() – Moves the turtle in forward direction

import turtle

x=turtle.Screen()

turtle.forward(200)

x.mainloop()

Output

D:\PythonPrograms>python a1.py

■ backward() – Moves the turtle in backward direction

import turtle

x=turtle.Screen()

turtle.backward(200)

x.mainloop()

Output

■ right() – Moves the turtle in right direction

import turtle

x=turtle.Screen()

turtle.right(100)

turtle.forward(100)

x.mainloop()

Output

■ left() – Moves the turtle in left direction

import turtle

x=turtle.Screen()

turtle.left(100)

turtle.forward(100)

x.mainloop()

Output

■ circle() – draws the circle shape

■ dot() – draws a dot shape

import turtle

x=turtle.Screen()

turtle.circle(100)

turtle.dot(100)

turtle.stamp()

x.mainloop()

Output

Glossary

■ Turtle module provides the facility for graphics in Python.

Summary

■ Python programming supports various standard libraries to handle different operations.

■ The os module is used for handling various operating system operations.

■ The difflib and textwrap modules are used for handling text processing operations.

■ Mathematical operations are handled with modules such as numbers, math, cmath, decimal, etc.

■ The zipfile module is used to handle data compression operations.

■ The graphics in Python is supported by turtle module.

CHAPTER

  

Testing

19

  

The process of software development includes several stages. After developing the code for the required product, the product will not be delivered directly to the client. If immediate delivery happens and the developed product failed, then it would lead to problems at the client’s end. Hence, the testing phase is used. In the testing phase, the developed code will be tested in various conditions. The advantage of testing is that it enables the developer to deliver an error-free product. The product will be delivered to the client only if the result of the developed product is satisfied for all test conditions. The present chapter discusses in detail about testing in Python.

19.1 Basic Concepts of Testing

The testing process evaluates the developed product on whether it satisfies all the given requirements. Testing can be performed in two different ways. They are

■ Manual testing

■ Computer based testing – It is also called as unit testing.

During the testing process, it is hard to terminate the program. For termination, various details should be considered such as deadline of testing, identification of completion of testing process, management decision, etc.

In software development life cycle, verification and validation processes are mainly used to estimate the quality of the product. The verification process is used to verify if the developed product meets all the requirements. In general this process is performed by the software developers. The validation process is used to validate the developed product by checking whether the product is defective. In general, the validation process is performed by software testers.

During the testing process, the bugs, errors or defects in the developed product will be identified. So, it is recommended to perform the testing process in the initial stages of the systems development life cycle (SDLC). This will also help to reduce the time and cost associated with testing the product. During the testing process, if the identified bugs, errors or defects are solved then it is called as debugging.

19.2 Unit Testing in Python

The unittest module should be imported to test a Python program. A test case represents individual unit of the test. A collection of text cases is called as test suite. The test runner will perform the testing process. The test fixture represents the requirements for performing a test. There are several functions in unittest module:

■ The unittest.TestCase() function is used to create a new test case.

■ The function with test_ as prefix represents the tests to be executed by the test runner.

■ The assertEqual() will check each test for the expected result.

■ The assertTrue() and assertFalse() verifies the condition for the test method.

■ The setup() and teardown() methods define the statements to be executed before and after the test method.

■ The unittest.main() provides command line interface.

import unittest

class t1(unittest.TestCase):

def test_starting_out(self):

self.assertEqual(1, 1)

if __name__ == “__main__”:

unittest.main()

Output

D:\PythonPrograms>python a1.py

.

-----------------------------------------------

Ran 1 test in 0.000s

OK

■ From command line also, the unit test module can be executed.

D:\PythonPrograms>python -m unittest

-----------------------------------------

Ran 0 tests in 0.000s

OK

■ For each test, the outcome can be in three different values, namely, OK, FAIL and ERROR. If the test method is succeeded without any bugs/errors then “PASS” will be returned. If the test method is failed then raises AssertionError exception. If the test method raises an exception, then “ERROR” will be returned.

class t1(unittest.TestCase):

def test_ok(self):

self.assertEqual(1,1)

def test_fail(self):

self.assertFalse(1<2)

def test_error(self):

raise RuntimeError(“Test ERROR”)

if __name__ == “__main__”:

unittest.main()

Output

D:\PythonPrograms>python a1.py

EF.

==============================

ERROR: test_error (__main__.t1)

-----------------------------------------------

Traceback (most recent call last):

File “a1.py”, line 11, in test_error

raise RuntimeError(“Test ERROR”)

RuntimeError: Test ERROR

==============================

FAIL: test_fail (__main__.t1)

-----------------------------------------------

Traceback (most recent call last):

File “a1.py”, line 8, in test_fail

self.assertFalse(1<2)

AssertionError: True is not false

-----------------------------------------------

Ran 3 tests in 0.000s

FAILED (failures=1, errors=1)

import unittest

In the above program, three test methods are defined, viz., {test_ok(), test_fail(), test_error()}. The test_ok() test method checks the condition. The condition is tested and the result is okay. The test_fail() test method checks the condition for false output. However, the test condition yields true result. So, the result will be AssertionError. The test_error() raises an exception called as RuntimeError.

Various methods applicable on the test case are listed below.

■ The assertEqual() checks whether or not the two input values.

■ The assertNotEqual() checks whether or not the two input values are not equal.

■ The assertTrue() checks if the result of the input condition is true or not.

■ The assertFalse() checks if the result of the input condition is false or not.

■ The assertRaises() checks whether the exception is raised or not.

■ The assertAlmostEqual() checks whether or not the two input values are almost similar.

■ The assertNotAlmostEqual() checks whether or not the two input values are not almost similar.

■ The assertRegrex() checks whether or not the regrex search result is matched with the text or not.

■ The assertIs() checks whether or not the first input is same as the second input.

■ The assertIsNot() checks whether or not the first input is not the same as the second input.

■ The assertIsNone() checks whether or not the input expression is none.

■ The assertIsNotNone() checks whether or not the input expression is not none.

■ The assertIn() checks whether or not the first input is in the second input.

■ The assertNotIn() checks whether or not the first input is not in the second input.

19.3 Grouping Test Cases in Unit Testing

In Python unit testing, multiple test cases can be grouped by using “test suite”. For this, TestSuite class in unit test module is used. The test case object is responsible for test whereas the test suite object is responsible for grouping multiple test case objects for test. Various methods in the test suite object are listed below.

■ The addTest() method will add a test case or test suite to the current test suite.

■ The addTests() method is used to add all the tests of the test case or test suite.

■ The run() will run the tests and store the result in input result object.

■ The countTestCases() counts the tests available in the test suite object.

19.4 Loading and Running Tests

In Python, multiple test cases are available in various classes and modules. All the test cases from these classes and modules can be loaded into a test suite by using TestLoader class. Various methods in this class are listed below.

■ The loadTestsFromTestCase() method will load all the test cases from the input class to the current test suite object.

■ The loadTestsFromModule() method will load all the test cases from the input module to the current test suite object.

■ The loadTestsFromName() method will load all the test cases from the input name to the current test suite object.

■ The getTestCaseNames() method will get the names of all test cases in the input class. The result will be in sorted order.

For running a test suite in Python, use the TextTestRunner() class. This class contains the run() method. The run() method will execute the input test suite.

import unittest

class t1(unittest.TestCase):

def testm1(self):

self.assertAlmostEqual(10,11)

def testm2(self):

self.assertNotAlmostEqual(10,11)

class t2(unittest.TestCase):

def testm3(self):

self.assertIsNone(none)

def testm4(self):

self.assertisNone(“hello”)

if __name__ == “__main__”:

l=unittest.TestLoader()

s1=unittest.TestSuite()

s2=unittest.TestSuite()

r=unittest.TextTestRunner()

s1.addTest(l.loadTestsFromTestCase(t1))

r.run(s1)

s2.addTest(l.loadTestsFromTestCase(t2))

r.run(s2)

Output

D:\PythonPrograms>python a1.py

F.

Glossary

■ The testing process evaluates the developed product on whether it satisfies all the given requirements.

■ Manual testing allows the user to perform the testing process manually.

■ Unit testing allows Python to perform the testing process.

Summary

■ Testing is an important step to validate the developed software product.

■ The testing procedure can be either manual or computer based.

■ The computer based testing procedure is called as unit testing.

■ Python programming supports unit testing feature.

■ Unit testing is supported by unittest module in Python.

■ New test cases can be created for the unit testing process and thus can be evaluated.

■ The result of the test case can be OK, FAILED or ERROR.

APPENDIX

  

Networking

A

  

A.1 Introduction

Networking allows multiple systems to communicate. Using this concept, multiple devices can be connected and the data can be shared among them. A network can be created among multiple devices as shown in Fig. 19.1.

Fig. 19.1 Network with three devices

Within a network, each and every device can be recognized with its IP address. The IP addresses are unique and no more than one device can have same IP address. In the network as shown in Fig. 19.1, all the three devices have unique IP addresses. Within the network, any device can communicate with another device. Protocols are designed to enable communication among devices in a network. In general, TCP/IP or UDP protocols are used to establish communication among the devices. For each device, the port is acts as an interface to get connected to other devices. In general, the port is represented by numbers. In the device, a program or service is represented by a port number. Not more than one program or service should use the same port number.

A.2 Networking in Python

Python programming also supports the designing of a network to establish communication. Python supports networking at two different levels. They are

■ Low-level networking

In low-level networking, the “socket” is used to create the network underlying the operating system.

■ High level networking

In high-level networking, communication is preformed with application-specific protocols such as HTTP, FTP, SMTP etc.

A.3 Low-level Networking

In Python, low-level networking is performed with the socket. Thus, the communication between client and server happens through the socket. The socket used for the client is “client socket” and the socket used for the server is called as “server socket”. A socket allows bidirectional communication.

In Python socket module provides several functions for low-level networking. So, in a low level networking python program, it is mandatory to use the statement import socket. Various functions of socket module are listed below.

■ socket()is used to create a new socket. It can be created by using the following syntax.

socket_name=socket.socket(socket_family, socket_type)

■ socket_family represents the type of the socket address family. The possible values for this are AF_UNIX and AF_INET. AF_UNIX is used for IPv4 protocol while AF_INET is used for IPv6 protocol. AF_UNIX is available only for UNIX-based operating system.

■ socket_type represents the type of the socket. There are two types of sockets, viz., SOCK_STREAM and SOCK_DGRAM. SOCK_STREAM is used to represent the connection based streams for TCP. SOCK_DGRAM is used to represent the datagram streams for UDP.

import socket

s1=socket.socket(socket.AF_INET,socket.SOCK_STREAM )

print(“Socket with AF_INET,SOCK_STREAM is created”)

s2=socket.socket(socket.AF_INET,socket.SOCK_DGRAM )

print(“Socket with AF_INET,SOCK_DGRAM is created”)

Output

D:\PythonPrograms>python a1.py

Socket with AF_INET, SOCK_STREAM is created

Socket with AF_INET, SOCK_DGRAM is created

■ socket.gethostname()will get the name of the host. It returns the local machine name.

import socket

print(“host name: “,socket.gethostname())

print(“host IPaddress: “,socket.gethostbyname(socket.gethostname()))

Output

D:\PythonPrograms>python a1.py

host name: DESKTOP-6ECS0BG

host IPaddress: 192.168.1.100

A.4 Client–Server Network

Client–server communication allows connecting multiple devices in a network. Among multiple devices, one device will act as “server” and the remaining devices will act as “clients”. The server is always the master controller or administrator of the entire network. Within the network, all the clients will be connected to the server. A client–server network is shown in Fig. 19.2.

Fig. 19.2 Client–server network

In a client–server network, communication is allowed between any client and the server. All the clients can communicate with the server. The server can also communicate with all the clients in the network. The server or the client can be identified by its unique IP address. Both, client and server, have ports for connecting with the external environment.

For communication between the client and the server, a “socket” is required. The socket should be created at the client side and the server side. The socket created for the client is called as “client socket” whereas the socket created for the server is called as “server socket”. After creating the socket at client or server side, the socket should be connected to the host and the port.

A.4.1. Server socket

The socket created at the server side is called as “server socket”. The server socket can be created by using socket(). The server socket can be with TCP socket or UDP socket. The host represents either the IP address or the name of the device. For this purpose, bind() method is used. With this, the socket can ‘listen’ to the communication received from the specific IP address and the port. The socket can also send the messages from the specific IP address and the port. The syntax for the bind()method is

socket_object.bind((host_del,port_num))

■ The socket_object represents the server socket object for which the bind() method should execute.

■ The host_del represents the name or IP address or the name of the host device.

■ The port_num is a number that represents the specific port.

import socket

s1=socket.socket(socket.AF_INET,socket.SOCK_STREAM )

print(“TCP Socket is created”)

s2=socket.socket(socket.AF_INET,socket.SOCK_DGRAM )

print(“UDP Socket is created”)

h=socket.gethostname()

p=255

s1.bind((h,p))

print(“TCP socket is binded”)

s2.bind((h,p))

print(“UDP socket is binded”)

Output

D:\PythonPrograms>python a2.py

TCP Socket is created

UDP Socket is created

TCP socket is binded

UDP socket is binded

In the above example code, the server socket is created and is connected to the local host device port 255. So, with this socket, the communication is allowed only to the local host device port 255.

After binding the server socket to the specific port of the host, the communication should be started between the client and the server socket. For this, initially the server socket should be connected with the client socket. The connection can be established only when the server socket receives a connection request from the client socket. So, the server socket should ‘listen’ for client socket requests. For this, listen()method is used. This method should be used only for server socket to receive the client socket requests. The syntax for the “listen()” method is

server_socket_object.listen(limit_req)

■ The server_socket_object represents the server socket object for which the listen() method should execute.

■ The limit_req represents the size of requests queue.

import socket

s1=socket.socket(socket.AF_INET,socket.SOCK_STREAM )

print(“TCP Socket is created”)

h=socket.gethostname()

p=255

s1.bind((h,p))

print(“TCP socket is binded”)

s1.listen(4)

print(“TCP socket listen upto 4 connection requests”)

Output

D:\PythonPrograms>python a2.py

TCP Socket is created

TCP socket is binded

TCP socket listen upto 4 connection requests

After the server socket is created, the server starts to listen for connection requests from the client. If it ‘hears’ any request, the server socket will accept the request and establish the connection with the requested client. For this accept()method is used. This method can be used only by the server socket. The syntax for the accept() method is

(client_socket,addr)=server_socket_object.accept()

■ The server_socket_object represents the server socket object for which the listen() method should execute.

■ The accept() method will establish the connection between the server socket and the client socket. It returns the client socket represented by client_socket and the address of the client socket, with addr.

A.4.2. Client socket

The client socket can be created by using the socket() method. After creating the client socket, it should be connected with the server socket. Communication is possible between the client and server sockets only when the connection is successful. In Python, the client–server socket can be connected to the server socket by using the following syntax.

client_socket_obj.connect((host_del,port_num))

■ The client_socket_object represents the client socket object for which the connect() method should execute.

■ The host_del represents the name or IP address or the name of the host device.

■ The port_num is a number that represents the specific port.

When the client socket calls the connect()method, internally the client socket sends a request to the server socket. If the server socket listens to the client’s connection requests, it will accept the request by using accept()and create a connection to the client socket. The example code below shows the usage of connect()method to connect between client and server sockets. In this example, both the sockets are created for the local host device. So, two command line interfaces are used for client and server sockets respectively.

a1.py

import socket

s1=socket.socket(socket.AF_INET,socket.SOCK_STREAM )

print(“Server Socket is created”)

h=socket.gethostname()

p=255

s1.bind((h,p))

print(“Server socket is binded”)

s1.listen(4)

print(“Server socket listen upto 4 connection requests”)

while True:

(c,a)=s1.accept()

print(“Request is received from the client”)

print(“Connected client address: “,a)

a2.py

import socket

s1=socket.socket(socket.AF_INET,socket.SOCK_STREAM )

print(“Client Socket is created”)

h=socket.gethostname()

p=255

print(“Client socket is sending connection request”)

s1.connect((h,p))

Output

A.5 Client–Server Communication

After establishing a connection between the client and server sockets, communication between them becomes allowed and messages are sent and received through the sockets. There are two types of sockets, viz., TCP and UDP. So, the communication between the sockets should be specific to the type of socket. In Python, various methods are available for handling communication between client and server sockets. They are listed below.

■ The recv()method is used to receive the message with TCP socket.

■ The send()method is used to send the message with TCP socket.

■ The recvfrom()method is used to receive the message with UDP socket.

■ The sendto()method is used to send the message with UDP socket.

■ The close() method is used to close the socket for which the object it is called.

a2.py

import socket

s1=socket.socket(socket.AF_INET,socket.SOCK_STREAM )

print(“Client Socket is created”)

h=socket.gethostname()

p=255

print(“Client socket is sending connection request”)

s1.connect((h,p))

m=s1.recv(1000)

print(m.decode(‘ascii’))

a1.py

import socket

s1=socket.socket(socket.AF_INET,socket.SOCK_STREAM )

print(“Server Socket is created”)

h=socket.gethostname()

p=255

s1.bind((h,p))

print(“Server socket is binded”)

s1.listen(4)

print(“Server socket listen upto 4 connection requests”)

while True:

(c,a)=s1.accept()

print(“Request is received from the client”)

print(“Connected client address: “,a)

t=”Hello from server”

c.send(t.encode(‘ascii’))

Output

A.6 High-level Networking

Python programming has various standard library modules to interact with internet services. This process is called as high-level networking. In this, the local host device will act as a client and connect to Web servers. There are several modules in Python for handling internet services such as urllib, HTTP, NNTP, FTP, SMTP and POP3.

In Python, File Transfer Protocol (FTP) is supported by the ftblib module. In this module, the FTP() is used to get connected to the FTP host and port. login() is used to perform the login operation with the username and password. cwd() is used to change the directory. retrlines() is used to list the contents of the current working directory. quit()is used to close the FTP connection. storbinary()is used to send the file from client to the FTP server.

httplib is used for handling the client-side operations of an HTTP connection. The smtplib is used to send email messages from client-side program.

APPENDIX

  

Sending E-mail

B

  

B.1 Introduction

Python programming provides high-level networking for handling internet services. It offers various libraries for supporting the different types of internet activities. Sending e-mail is a popular internet service that is supported by Python. In Python high-level networking, the local host device will act as client session object and connect to the requested mail server. When connection is established between client and the mail server, the client session object will be able to send mails. Python uses Simple Mail Transfer Protocol (SMTP) for sending the mails from one account to other account. The smtplib module is used for handling the mail sending process.

B.2 Creation of SMTP Object

Python has smtplib module for handling objects of SMTP. For creating an SMTP object, SMTP()function is used. The syntax is given below.

new_smtp_obj=smtp.SMTP(host_del,port_num)

■ host_del represents the name of the SMTP mail server.

■ port_num represents the port of the SMTP mail server for which the communication should be established.

■ new_smtp_obj is the SMTP object created and returned by the SMTP() function.

import smtplib

try:

s1 = smtplib.SMTP(‘smtp.mail.yahoo.com’,587)

print(“SMTP object is created”)

except SMTPException:

print(“Error: unable to use SMTP”)

Output

D:\PythonPrograms>python a2.py

SMTP object is created

B.3 Mail Server Connection

After creating the SMTP object, a connection to the SMTP mail server is required. This connection is created by the connect()method. With this method, the connection between client side and the SMTP mail server side is established. The syntax for the connect()method is given below.

smtp_obj.conenct(host_del,port_num)

■ smtp_obj represents the SMTP object for which the connection is established.

■ host_del represents the name of the SMTP mail server.

■ port_num represents the port of the SMTP mail server for which the communication should be established.

import smtplib

try:

s1 = smtplib.SMTP(‘smtp.mail.yahoo.com’,587)

print(“SMTP object is created”)

s1.connect(‘smtp.mail.yahoo.com’,587)

print(“connected to mail.yahoo.com server via SMTP”)

except SMTPException:

print(“Error: unable to use SMTP”)

Output

D:\PythonPrograms>python a2.py

SMTP object is created

connected to mail.yahoo.com server via SMTP

B.4 Login to Mail Server

After creating the connection between the client side and the SMTP mail server side, the user will be able to login to the mail account by providing the username and password. For this, login( ) method should be used.

import smtplib

try:

s1 = smtplib.SMTP(‘smtp.mail.yahoo.com’,587)

print(“SMTP object is created”)

s1.connect(‘smtp.mail.yahoo.com’,587)

print(“Connected to mail.yahoo.com server via SMTP”)

s1.ehlo()

s1.starttls()

s1.ehlo()

s1.login(“xxx@yahoo.com”,”xxx”)

print(“Login is successful”)

except SMTPException:

print(“Error: unable to use SMTP”)

Output

D:\PythonPrograms>python a2.py

SMTP object is created

Connected to mail.yahoo.com server via SMTP

Login is successful

B.5 Sending E-mail

After logging into the SMTP mail server, e-mails can be sent from the user’s account. The message can be sent by using sendmail()method. The syntax for the method is

ser_obj.sendmail(from_add,to_add,msg)

■ ser_obj represents the server object for which the mail should be sent.

■ from_add represents the From address.

■ to_add represents the To address.

■ mg represents the message delivered.

import smtplib

try:

s1 = smtplib.SMTP(‘smtp.mail.yahoo.com’,587)

print(“SMTP object is created”)

s1.connect(‘smtp.mail.yahoo.com’,587)

print(“connected to mail.yahoo.com server via SMTP”)

s1.ehlo()

s1.starttls()

s1.ehlo()

s1.login(“xxx@yahoo.com”,”xxx”))

print(“login is successful”)

fad=” xxx@yahoo.com “

taddr=”yyy@yahoo.com”

msg = “This mail is sent from python”

s1.sendmail(fad, taddr, msg)

s1.quit()

print(“mail sent successfully”)

except SMTPException:

print(“Error: unable to use SMTP”)

Output

D:\PythonPrograms>python a2.py

SMTP object is created

connected to mail.yahoo.com server via SMTP

login is successful

mail sent successfully

B.6 E-mail Package

The “email” package in Python can also send and parse e-mail messages. For this, two import statements should be used. The message can be a MIMEMultipart object. The message should be loaded with various headers, viz., “From”, “To” and “Subject” and appended to the body of the mail. The complete message can be transferred using the SMTP server object.

import smtplib

from email.MIMEMultipart import MIMEMulipart

from email.MIMEText import MIMEText

try:

s1 = smtplib.SMTP(‘smtp.mail.yahoo.com’,587)

print(“SMTP object is created”)

s1.connect(‘smtp.mail.yahoo.com’,587)

print(“Connected to mail.yahoo.com server via SMTP”)

s1.ehlo()

s1.starttls()

s1.ehlo()

s1.login(“xxx@yahoo.com”,”xxx”))

print(“Login is successful”)

fad=” xxx@yahoo.com “

taddr=»yyy@yahoo.com»

m=MIMEMultipart()

m[‘From’]=fad

m[‘To’]=taddr

m[‘subject’]=python mail

b = “This mail is sent from python”

m.attach(MIMEText(b,’plain’))

t=m.as_string()

s1.sendmail(fad, taddr, t)

s1.quit()

print(“Mail sent successfully”)

except SMTPException:

print(“Error: unable to use SMTP”)

Output

D:\PythonPrograms>python a2.py

SMTP object is created

Connected to mail.yahoo.com server via SMTP

Login is successful

Mail sent successfully

from plotly import __version__

from plotly.offline import download_plotlyjs, init_notebook_mode, plot, iplot

D:\PythonPrograms>python s1.py

In offline mode, graphs can be plotted and saved in the local system. There are two types of plotting in offline mode. They are

■ With plotly.offline.plot()

■ With plotly.offline.iplot()

C.3 Plotting with plotly.offline.plot()

plot() is used to plot the graph and save it in a local standalone HTML file. iplot() is used to plot the graph in a Jupyter notebook file. The HTML file will be opened in the local web browser and the Jupyter file will be opened in the notebook.

import plotly

from plotly.graph_objs import Scatter, Layout

plotly.offline.plot({

“data”: [Scatter(x=[10, 20, 30, 40], y=[40, 30, 20, 10])],

“layout”: Layout(title=”with plot()”)

})

Output

C.4 Plotting with plotly.offline.iplot()

For this mode of plotting graphs, initially the ipython is installed with all modes by using the following command.

pip install “ipython[all]”

Then install the Jupyter notebook App by using the following command.

jupyter notebook

With this command, the Jupyter notebook is openned as shown below.

To create a new notebook, click on New → Python3

Type the code and click Run

import plotly

from plotly.offline import init_notebook_mode, iplot

from plotly.graph_objs import *

init_notebook_mode(connected=True)

trace0 = Scatter(

x=[1, 2, 3, 4],

y=[10, 15, 13, 17]

)

trace1 = Scatter(

x=[1, 2, 3, 4],

y=[16, 5, 11, 9]

)

data = Data([trace0, trace1])

plotly.offline.iplot(data,filename = ‘basic-line’)

Output

C.4.1 Bar graph

The bar graph can be plotted with Bar() function. It requires two arguments for x and y axes.

import plotly

from plotly.offline import init_notebook_mode, iplot, plot

import plotly.graph_objs as pg

init_notebook_mode(connected=True)

k=[‘first’,’second’,’third’]

v=[10,20,30]

plotly.offline.iplot({

“data”: [pg.Bar(x=k, y=v)],

“layout”: pg.Layout(title=”Bar Graph”)

})

Output

The box graph can be plotted with Box() function. It requires two arguments for x and y axes.

import plotly

from plotly.offline import init_notebook_mode, iplot, plot

import plotly.graph_objs as pg

init_notebook_mode(connected=True)

k=[‘first’,’second’,’third’]

v=[10,20,30]

“data”: [pg.Box(x=k, y=v)],

“layout”: pg.Layout(title=”Box Graph”)

})

The heatmap graph can be plotted with Heatmap() function. It requires a matrix argument.

import plotly

import plotly.graph_objs as pg

init_notebook_mode(connected=True)

plotly.offline.iplot({

C.4.4 Histogram graph

The histogram graph can be plotted with Histogram() function. It requires array value as an argument.

import plotly

from plotly.offline import init_notebook_mode, iplot, plot

import plotly.graph_objs as pg

import numpy as np

init_notebook_mode(connected=True)

x = np.random.randn(20)

plotly.offline.iplot({

“data”: [pg.Histogram(x=x)],

“layout”: pg.Layout(title=”Vertical Histogram Graph”)

})

plotly.offline.iplot({

“data”: [pg.Histogram(y=x)],

“layout”: pg.Layout(title=”Horizontal Histogram Graph”)

})

The pie graph can be plotted with Pie() function. It requires array label and value as arguments.

import plotly

import plotly.graph_objs as pg

import numpy as np

init_notebook_mode(connected=True)

x = [10,30,20,15,25]

y=[‘a’,’b’,’c’,’d’,’e’]

plotly.offline.iplot({

“data”: [pg.Pie(labels=y, values=x)],

“layout”: pg.Layout(title=”Pie Graph”)

})

APPENDIX

  

CGI/ Web Programming using Python

D

  

D.1 Introduction

Common Gateway Interface (CGI) is a standard protocol used to exchange information between the user script file and Web server. With this protocol, the Web pages can be dynamically generated at the Web server. These pages are generated by using CGI scripts. The CGI scripts are executed in the Web browser. The Web browser sends the requested CGI script name to the Web server, where it will be executed by the Web server and the response of the CGI script is transferred to the web browser.

D.2 Configuration of Apache Server

To execute Python-based CGI scripts, the following steps are required.

■ The Python installation directory is

D:\Python\Python36-32

■ Install the Web server in the system. In the present section we consider Tomcat Web server installed by using XAMPP. The path of the XAMPP installation directory is

C:\xampp

■ In “xampp” folder, go to “apache” folder.

■ In “apache” folder, go to “conf” directory.

■ To configure the Apache web server and run the Python CGI scripts, add the following lines of code at the end of the httpd.conf file.

AddHandler cgi-script .py

ScriptInterpreterSource Registry-Strict

■ Then open “xampp” control panel and restart the “Apache”

D.3 Running Python CGI Script with Apache Server

After configuring the Apache web server with the Python CGI script feature, perform the following steps.

■ Create a folder (PP) in the “htdocs” folder.

C:\xampp\htdocs\PP

■ In this folder, create a Python CGI script. The first line of the script should be the path of Python.

C:\xampp\htdocs\PP\s1.py

#!D:/Python/Python36-32/python.exe

print(«Content-Type: text/html\n»)

print (“Hello World!”)

■ Open the web browser, and type the following URL.

http://localhost/PP/s1.py

Output

D.4 Python CGI Environment Variables

With Python CGI script, the environment variables of the operating system can be accessed. For this, os module should be used. The os module allows the Python script to interact with the operating system.

#!D:/Python/Python36-32/python.exe

import os

print(«Content-Type: text/html\n»)

print(«<h1>CGI Environment Variables </h1><br>»)

for p in os.environ.keys():

print(p,»:»,os.environ[p],»</br>»)

Output

D.5 Form Handling with Python CGI Script

Data can be transferred between the Web browser and the Web server. Form data can be communicated by using either “post” or “get” methods (used to insert/update or retrieve remote data, respectively). The sample code below shows the form handling with Python CGI script.

■ Create a form a.html which reads the name of the user. For this, the form contains a textbox and a button.

C:\xampp\htdocs\PP\a.html

<form action = “/PP/s1.py” method = “POST”>

First Name: <input type = “text” name = “name”> </br>

<input type = “submit” value = “Click” />

</form>

■ Then, write the Python CGI script, which receives the form data by using “POST” or “GET”. The current example uses “POST” method. The CGI module in Python is used to handle form data with “POST” or “GET” requests. The FieldStorage()function in CGI module receives the data from the form.

C:\xampp\htdocs\PP\s1.py

#!D:/Python/Python36-32/python.exe

import cgi

f=cgi.FieldStorage()

name=f.getvalue(‘name’)

print(“Content-Type: text/html\n”)

print(“Hello”,name)

Output
